- Rebuilt core: prisma schema (Event/Credential/IngestToken/Setting) + db:push; src/lib/crypto.ts (AES-256-GCM vault, v1 envelope, VaultIntegrityError, ENCRYPTION_KEYS_PREV); src/lib/auth.ts (PANEL_PASSWORD, HMAC cookie sessions, requireSession); src/lib/guard.ts (rate limit).

Stage Summary:
- Core L0 + panel skeleton + theme + auth + vault live on :3000, 18/18 verified.
- Owner gets password by downloading workspace → .env → PANEL_PASSWORD (runbook §0).
- Wave-2 (7-a Upstash, 7-b Hyperbrowser, 7-c Webshare, 7-d GitHub+Supabase) launching next; connectors.tsx placeholder replaced on integration.

- upstash-card.tsx: self-contained TanStack Query card (key ["connector","upstash","status"], 30s re-probe); NOT CONFIGURED form (url + password token inputs, h-11) / SAVING / CONNECTED health row (mono measured latency + MEASURED badge, refresh, disconnect) / UNHEALTHY typed-error row; skeleton loading, toasts per action, aria labels + role="status", 44px targets, token classes only (no blue/indigo).
- tests/verify-t204.sh: P7-safe — 17 structural checks: unauth 401 ×3, input validation 400 ×2, fake-cred vault roundtrip with echo-leak greps, honest unhealthy probe, delete + configured:false + idempotency.

Stage Summary:
- T-204 code-complete [MEASURED]: verify-t204.sh 17/17 PASS (exit 0); bun run lint CLEAN; dev.log shows only expected 401/400/200 on new routes, no errors.
- Live probe verified end-to-end with fake endpoint (healthy:false + typed sanitized error, no url/token in responses) [MEASURED]. Real-Upstash success path (/ping PONG, /pipeline shapes, 401/429) implemented per REST docs knowledge [INFERRED]/[UNVERIFIED] — no real credentials in sandbox; owner pastes creds via panel and the probe reports truth.
- Integration note for orchestrator: import { UpstashCard } from "@/components/panel/connectors/upstash-card" into the Connectors tab; full API contract in agent-ctx/7-a-upstash-connector.md.

PANEL_PASSWORD read from .env, never echoed.
- bun run lint CLEAN; tsc --noEmit: no errors outside owner upload/ dir (pre-existing, outside build scope); verify-core.sh re-run 18/18 (no regression); dev.log clean, status probe render ~900ms ≈ real outbound RTT.

- Root causes found for "entered GitHub connection successfully, but that is also problematic" [MEASURED/INFERRED]:
  1. exchangeCodeForToken sent `process.env.GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` directly — a vault-only setup would have exchanged with an empty secret → guaranteed failure. Now resolves from the vault like the id.

No mirror path: the rebuilt panel wrote the token only to the vault, but scripts/snapshot-backup.sh reads ops/vault/github.token → a panel-only connection would have silently starved the 6h durability cadence (exit 3 NO_TOKEN). storeGitHubToken now atomically mirrors (tmp+rename, 0600, dir 0700; failure is best-effort + logged as github.token_mirror_failed with fs code only).
  4. Scope was `repo read:user` — far broader than needed. Downgraded to `read:user public_repo` (identity probe + backup push only).
  5. Device-flow poll status "error" was never surfaced client-side and polling never stopped (silent infinite poll on e.g. credential rejection). Card now stops + toasts the honest message; server classifies unknown-client_id as APP_CREDENTIALS_REJECTED.

Work Log:

Two unlocks: (1) sitemap https://docs.kickbot.com/dashboard/sitemap.xml → sitemap-pages.xml enumerates every doc page; (2) ANY page exports clean markdown by appending `.md` (e.g. /dashboard/widgets/widget-builder-docs.md). - DASHBOARD URL MAP (IA to mirror): /dashboard/commands, /dashboard/timer, /dashboard/clipcommand, /dashboard/tts/commands (+ library/moderation/settings tabs), /dashboard/moderation, /dashboard/character, /dashboard/imagine, /dashboard/widget-library, /dashboard/saved-widgets, /dashboard/chatalerts, /dashboard/tipping, kickbot.com/voices/{channel}, kickbot.com/clips/{channel}.

PAGE 1 — WIDGET BUILDER (docs.kickbot.com/dashboard/widgets/widget-builder-docs)
- What it does: program custom overlay widgets in HTML+CSS+JS, rendered in an OBS browser-source page; integrates with stream events through a KickBot JS runtime. Advanced tool, gated behind a "requires programming knowledge" note; pre-made alternative = Widget Library. New widget = blank template OR preset template (example: "Simple follower goal" ships as template + library widget). Free plan cap: 10 widgets.
- UI STRUCTURE: main areas = LEFT SIDEBAR / CODE EDITOR / PREVIEW / EVENTS doc. Sidebar sections: (1) Basic settings — Name (label), Dimensions (px WxH, default+recommended 800x600). (2) User variables — end-user-adjustable settings w/o touching code; accessed in JS as `userVariables.variable_name.value`; per-variable fields: Title (user-facing label), Variable name (code key, no spaces/special chars), Tooltip (hover help), Variable type (enum: text, number, color, dropdown, toggle, image/GIF, video, audio — media types return a hosted-storage URL; file uploads ≤25MB via 2-step upload API), Default value (may be empty), Required (bool). (3) Send test events — buttons firing fake events with real event structure. Code editor: full HTML/CSS/JS, movable/resizable, layout buttons top-right. Preview: realtime auto-reload, rendered at configured resolution (scaled), Reload + Pop-out buttons; TIP: pop out to inspect console. 
- SAFETY MODEL (documented): chat text is sanitized before events — invisible char injected after `<` when text could start an HTML tag, event-handler names like `onerror=` broken, invisible char before `:` in executable URL schemes (`javascript:`); applies to data.content, data.sender.username, pinned messages, globalValues.chatMessages, gift+raid message text. Safe helpers: window.kickbotWidget.createTextNode(text), createChatMessageFragment(content, fontSize) (text nodes + Kick emote images, never HTML), escapeHtmlText(value) (for between-tags text only, never URL/CSS/script). Never put event fields into innerHTML.
- EVENTS (single listener pattern): document.addEventListener('widgetEvent', e => e.detail.{event_name, data}). Documented events w/ payload examples: subEvent {months, username, chatroom_id}; subGiftEvent {gifted_usernames[], gifter_username}; followEvent / unfollowEvent {user.slug/username, followers_count, created_at}; chatMessageEvent {id, chatroom_id, content, type, created_at, sender{id, username, slug, identity{color, badges[{type,text}]}}}; chatMessageDeletedEvent {message.id}; chatMessagePinnedEvent {full message object}; chatMessageUnpinnedEvent; chatClearedEvent; followerCountUpdatedEvent {followersCount, channel_id, created_at}; viewerCountUpdatedEvent {viewer_count, channel_id}; raidEvent {number_viewers, host_username, optional_message}; streamOnline/streamOffline {livestream{session_title, created_at}}. API ref adds: kicksEvent, subCountUpdatedEvent.
- GLOBAL VALUES (initialized on page load, auto-updated): globalValues = {followerCount, viewerCount, subCount (API ref), lastFollower, lastSub, lastGiftedSub, chatMessages[], streamerInfo.kick{username, slug, profile_picture, user_id, channel_id, chatroom_id, bio, subscriber_badges[]}, currentStream{is_live, title, started_at}}.
- VARIABLES/TEMPLATING: JS-side only — userVariables.<name>.value + globalValues.<name>. No string placeholder templating in widgets. Full example widget (follower goal) documented: HTML skeleton, CSS with CSS custom properties driven from userVariables colors via setProperty, JS event-handler map + init from globalValues.
- Example code shipped in docs: event handler map pattern, progress-bar math, init-from-globals pattern — reusable as our widget-builder starter template.

PAGE 2 — AI IMAGES WIDGET (/dashboard/widgets/ai-images-widget)
- What it does: `!imagine <prompt>` chat command → AI image generated → slides onto stream via browser-source widget; optional chat reply with direct image link. Dashboard at kickbot.com/dashboard/imagine.
- UI STRUCTURE: page layout = one main **Command Settings** card + **Widget URL** box + live **Widget Preview** + right-side **usage counter**. Setup steps: 1) toggle **Enabled** top-right of card, 2) **Who Can Use** roles, 3) widget options + on-screen time, 4) **Save Changes**, 5) copy Widget URL → OBS/Streamlabs browser source. 
- Who Can Use: 4 role toggles — Everyone (default, overrides others), Subs, VIPs, Mods; freely mixable; streamer always allowed regardless.
- Widget Options (toggles): **Show command example** ("Generate images with !imagine" header on widget; on by default), **Respond with URL** (bot replies with direct image link; on by default).
- Widget Settings: **On-screen time** slider 5–300s (default 60); **Transition** enum: None, Fade, Slide from left, Slide from right, Slide from bottom (default), Slide from top. Hint: live settings changes need browser-source refresh.
- Test: **Send test image to OBS** button pushes a sample (flamingo lounge) through the real path; appears within ~1s. Widget Preview mirrors settings live.
- USAGE LIMITS: free 50 images / 30 days, premium 1,000 / 30 days; ROLLING window (last 30d from now); only successful generations count; role-excluded messages don't count; counter in right sidebar.
- Troubleshooting checklist (order matters): KickBot must be Kick-channel mod → Enabled toggle → Who Can Use → reload browser source → usage meter.
- API field table adds (Premium/style): show_command (viewer name + prompt under image, default true), url_response, on_screen_time 5-300, slide_direction none|fade|left|right|bottom|top, **style** enum none|anime|film|cartoon|bricks|animated_3d|clay|pixel|comic|photo|watercolor|oil|neon (style prompt guideline; Premium; free always "none"); POST /imagine/test (rate 20/min, test images don't count); GET /imagine/queue?minutes=1-1440 (recent images oldest-first = what widget is showing).

PAGE 3 — KICK VIEWER WIDGET (/dashboard/widgets/kick-viewer-widget)
- What it does: realtime Kick viewer-count overlay from the Widget Library; same install flow for ALL library widgets (documented as the canonical 3-step flow: install → customize → paste URL in OBS).
- UI STRUCTURE: (1) **Widget Library** page (kickbot.com/dashboard/widget-library): grid of tiles sorted by installs (default), search box to filter ("viewer" → Viewer Count + Text Viewer Count; "goal" → follower/sub goals). (2) Detail page: preview thumbnails LEFT (one per icon option), **Install Widget** button RIGHT → becomes "Saved Widgets" link after install; install = personal copy (original untouched, can install again). (3) **Saved Widgets** page (kickbot.com/dashboard/saved-widgets): cards each with 4 actions — **Edit**, **URL**, **Clone**, trash (delete). (4) Editor popup: settings LEFT, live preview RIGHT (previews against current count); fields: **Widget name** (list label only), **Icon** enum: Kick (K logo, default) | People | Person | Eye, **Background color** picker/hex (default black), **Text color** (default white). **Save Changes** DISABLED until dirty; **Reset to Default** restores fields without saving. **Test Events** accordion: "Viewer count updated" fake event bumps preview to e.g. 3,430 — editor preview only, never on stream. **OBS Integration** accordion: browser-source URL (eye icon reveal, Click-to-copy), recommended source size 220x100. **URL button** on card opens popup with same URL + OBS instructions. Security warning: treat URL like a password (leak → delete + reinstall for fresh URL).
- Behavior: count updates a few times/min; 0 when offline. Second style in library: **Text Viewer Count** = plain text "Viewers: 3,372" (no pill). 
- API text-templating version (viewer-count overlay): fields text (with {{viewer_count}} placeholder), font_size 10-40 (default 32), boldness 401-700 (default 600), text_color hex, font enum of 10 (open sans, orbitron, VT323, Silkscreen, Amatic SC, Codystar, Rampart One, Delicious Handrawn, Eater, creepster), animate bool (count up/down smoothly, default true).

PAGE 4 — AI TEXT-TO-SPEECH (/dashboard/chat-bot/ai-text-to-speech)
- What it does: viewers send `!<voice> <message>`; audio plays through TTS browser source; 3,000+ AI voices (3,491 currently), voice clones, AI moderation, on-screen captions. Dashboard = 4 TABS: Commands | Voice Library | Moderation | Settings. Entry: kickbot.com/dashboard/tts/commands or "TTS Commands" in left sidebar.
- COMMANDS TAB (instant-save — no Save button; "changes save the moment you toggle or click away"): layout = saved voices LEFT, **AI Voice Clones** panel RIGHT. Each voice card: voice name + icon, chat command box (`!` prefix fixed; editable token: letters+digits ≤15; Enter or blur to save), **Who Can Use** row (All default + overrides; off → combine VIP/Sub/Mod), enable toggle (right), speaker icon (voice preview), delete button. **Sort menu**: enabled first / disabled first / alphabetical (preference remembered). **Bulk Edit**: tick several voices → pick permission set → **Apply**. Default 4 free voices: Brian, Female, Male, TikTok TTS (commands !brian, !tts, !tts2, !tiktok). Premium: full library + 10 clone slots.
- VOICE LIBRARY TAB: full catalogue (3,491 voices), sort **Most Popular** | **Recently Added** | **Trending**, search by name; each row: play-button preview + **Add** button (no cap on added voices; non-free need Premium to enable). API filters: category enum (streamers, musicians, athletes, politicians, actors_tv, cartoons, anime, video_games, tv_movie_characters, memes, narrators), language ISO 639-1, gender male/female, sort popular|most_installed|newest|trending; fields installs, messages_sent, AI tags (categories, gender, voice_language, identity).
- VOICE CLONING (Premium, 10 slots): **Create New Voice** popup fields: Voice name ≤20 chars; Default command ≤20 chars (no `!`); Voice icon = square image with built-in cropper; Audio samples = 1–5 clips, 10–90s total (30s recommended), MP3/WAV/M4A ≤10MB each, clean single-speaker speech; **Make voice public** optional toggle (manual review 1–2 business days + email on completion); **Rights confirmation** checkbox REQUIRED (no consent = account termination risk). Training ≤1 min, keep page open; finished voice lands at top of command list. API: 4-step flow (create → PUT uploads (audio 15 MIME types ≤10MiB, icon image ≤5MiB) → train → finalize 10-20s; new clone gets a disabled command; AI tags applied in background).
- MODERATION TAB (pre-play filters): toggles **Bad Words**, **Repeated Text**, **Block URLs**; **AI Content Filters** row of 8: Sexual Content, Hate Speech, Violence, Harassment, Illicit Content, Self Harm, Gibberish, Bad Username; textareas **Custom Banned Words** (1/line, case-insensitive), **Banned Usernames** (blocks viewer from TTS entirely), **Regex Patterns** (1/line, standard syntax); **Moderated Messages** panel = last 50 blocked messages live WITH reason per row. API fields: bad_word_filter, excessive_repeat_filter, url_filter, custom_bad_words, custom_regex, banned_usernames (strings, newline-separated), ai_filter_{sexual,hate,violence,harassment,illicit,self_harm,gibberish,offensive_name}.
- SETTINGS TAB (explicit **Save Changes**): **TTS Enabled** master switch in card header (off = nothing plays); **Automatic TTS** = reads every chat message via one of 4 built-in voices (Multilingual Male, Multilingual Female, English-only Male, English-only Female), NOT affected by cooldowns; **Read Sender Name**; **On-Screen Preview** caption + **Customize** popup (position, animation, font size, text color, background opacity, show sender, show voice name — API: position center|top|bottom, animation none|fade|slide-left|slide-right|slide-top|slide-bottom, text_size 8-48 default 24, text_color hex, background bool, background_opacity 0-100, show_sender, show_voice); **Voice List** = public page kickbot.com/voices/yourchannel (enabled voices + leaderboard; also `!voices` in chat); **Delay** slider 0-30s; **User Cooldown** 0-120s; **Global Cooldown** 0-120s; **Volume** 0-100%; **Max Characters** 0-300 (default 300; over-cap = skipped not truncated). **Widget URL** box: https://kickbot.com/external/<your-id>/tts → OBS browser source, tick "Control audio via OBS"; invisible unless captions on. Right sidebar = 6 LIVE CHAT COMMANDS (streamer or any mod): !ttsoff (queue continues), !ttson, !ttsclear (queue only, current keeps playing), !ttsskip, !ttsvol 0-100, !ttsdelay 0-30.
- AUTO-READ RULES ENGINE (API, the modern "Automatic TTS"): enabled, read_everyone, voice_id (any voice; null = legacy), max_reads_per_minute 1-30, read_sender, min_chars 0-300, max_chars 1-300, skip_links (false = strip link, read rest), skip_emote_only, skip_bots (BotRix/Kicklet/Lumia + bot badge), ignored_viewers ≤100. RULES ordered: viewer ("user") rules first, then badge rules (broadcaster, moderator, vip, og, founder, subscriber w/ min_months, sub_gifter, verified, staff, sidekick), then fallback voice; reorder endpoint; first match wins; "Do not read" = voice_id null.
- Troubleshooting order: TTS Enabled + Save → voice enable + roles → Max Characters → OBS refresh cache → audio monitoring (Advanced Audio Properties → Monitor and Output) → Moderated Messages panel for block reasons.

PAGE 5 — MODERATION (/dashboard/chat-bot/moderation)
- What it does: background auto-delete of bad chat messages (pre-delivery) + Moderation Logs of everything caught. Runs only on regular viewers — STREAMER AND KICK MODERATORS ARE IMMUNE to every filter (surfaces as right-sidebar callout).
- UI STRUCTURE: single page. **Moderation Filters** card = 5 independent toggles: **Bad Word Filter** (built-in profanity list), **Repeated Text** (long runs of same char/phrase), **Excessive Caps (>70%)** (auto-labeled threshold), **Zalgo Text** (stacked combining marks), **Message Length Limit** (OFF by default; on → 0-500 char cap). Then two textareas: **Custom Banned Words** (1/line; contains-match; ≥2 chars, ≤100 entries [API says ≤200 kept], shorter/extra silently dropped on save; example: list catches "check out spamword.io" but not "spam"), **Regex Patterns** (1/line, ≤20 patterns; nested quantifiers `(a+)+`/`(a*)+` REJECTED on save (catastrophic backtracking guard); non-compiling lines dropped, rest saved; examples: discord\.gg/\w+, bit\.ly/\w+, (buy|sell)\s+followers). **Save Changes** button + success toast (docs warn: "a toggle reverting silently means the page never saved"). **Moderation Logs** card under filters: rows = chatter username, triggered filter, original message, timestamp; scroll to load more; honest empty state ("normal until filters catch something live").
- API: GET/PATCH /moderation/settings (bad_word_filter, repeated_text_filter, excessive_caps_filter, zalgo_filter, max_message_length int ≥1, 500 = no limit, custom_banned_words array, banned_regex array), GET /moderation/logs?limit=1-100&offset (newest first, reason + message).

PAGE 6 — AI CHARACTER (/dashboard/chat-bot/ai-character)
- What it does: bot replies in persona when a viewer pings @KickBot in chat. One persona active at a time; switching applies to replies after save.
- UI STRUCTURE: single **Character Settings** card + **How It Works** sidebar. Steps: 1) **Enabled** toggle top-right; 2) **Who Can Use** (4 role toggles, Everyone default + overrides, mixable, streamer always); 3) **Character Persona**: 7 presets — Default (friendly helpful), Darth Vader, Donald Trump, Harry Potter, Andrew Tate, Giga Chad, Catgirl — plus **Custom** (opens prompt textarea; validated 15–1,000 chars on save; guidance copy: "Be specific about voice, tone, and any rules"; longer prompts = more consistent replies); 4) **AI Model** picker, 4 options with knowledge-cutoff captions: Grok 4.1 (xAI, default, Dec 2024), Gemini 2.5 (Google, Jan 2025), DeepSeek V3.2 (Sep 2025), GPT-4o (OpenAI, Oct 2023); warning hint: no live info/cutoffs. 5) **Save Changes**.
- USAGE: free = 100 replies / 30 days ROLLING, meter in right sidebar (counts only replies actually posted); premium = unlimited, meter hidden.
- Test: ping `@KickBot how is your day going?` from own chat. Troubleshooting: channel mod → Who Can Use → Enabled → usage → message must start with @KickBot.
- API (newer than doc page — includes drift): character enum has 16 presets (adds Pirate Captain, Hype Announcer, Grumpy Grandpa, Medieval Knight, Robot, Mob Boss, Wise Wizard, Cowboy); model ids grok|gpt|claude|gemini|deepseek|kimi (free locked to grok; model_options in GET); **web_search** boolean (live web lookups, Premium, on when channel upgrades); access via everyone_only/subscriber_only/vip_only/mod_only booleans.

VARIABLES SYSTEM (HIGH VALUE — chat-bot/custom-command-variables + command editor "Show Variables" panel)
- SYNTAX: double-curly placeholders `{{namespace.field}}` and function calls `{{fn(args)}}` inside command Response (≤500 chars). NOTE the doc page itself has broken braces on some entries (`{{sender.followers}` with one closing brace) — treat `{{...}}` as canonical (API + setup guide confirm).
- SENDER (11): {{sender.username}}, {{sender.followers}}, {{sender.followage}}, {{sender.url}}, {{sender.bio}}, {{sender.instagram}}, {{sender.twitter}}, {{sender.youtube}}, {{sender.discord}}, {{sender.tiktok}}, {{sender.facebook}} (socials pulled from the sender's Kick profile).

FINDINGS PER REPO:

1) sa-tokens/sa-token-rust — Rust auth/token framework (port of Java sa-token).
- Architecture: workspace split sa-token-core / sa-token-adapter (SaStorage + SaRequest/SaResponse abstractions) / sa-token-macro (#[sa_check_login], #[sa_check_permission("user:delete")], #[sa_ignore]) / sa-token-storage-{memory,redis,database(placeholder)} / 9+ web-framework plugin crates (Axum, Actix-web, Poem, Rocket, Warp, Salvo, Tide, Gotham, Ntex). Multi-version strategy: single `sa-token-plugin-*` façade crate + Cargo features pick framework major line (v4/v05…), with shared HTTP-agnostic `-core` pipeline crates; framework-agnostic path-auth pipeline (PathAuthConfig, run_auth_flow) lives in core and is consumed by every versioned middleware — write auth logic ONCE, bind per framework version.
- Token lifecycle: 6 token styles (UUID, SimpleUuid, Random32/64/128, JWT, Hash, Timestamp, Tik short-tokens for URL/QR sharing) chosen per config; storage pluggable with swappable serializer (JSON default, optional fory binary); refresh-token manager (generate/store/refresh_access_token); NonceManager (5-min TTL, validate_and_consume one-shot) for replay prevention; event listener bus (async_trait SaTokenListener: on_login/on_logout/on_kick_out; built-in LoggingListener).
- Higher-level flows: full OAuth2 authorization-code (register client → generate_authorization_code → exchange_code_for_token → verify_access_token → refresh); ticket-based SSO (SsoServer with ticket_timeout=300s, SsoClient.login_by_ticket, validate_ticket, unified logout fanning out to all client URLs, cross-domain config with allow-listed origins); multi-account isolation via StpLogic per login_type ("admin" vs "user" — same login_id gets fully isolated tokens+permissions); multi-device terminal tracking (login_with_device("PC"/"APP"), get_terminal_list filterable by device type, reverse-lookup terminal BY TOKEN, logout removes exactly that terminal); online-user registry + WebSocket auth with multiple token sources (Header/Cookie/Query + Bearer).
- NEXUS borrow: our requireSession is a single HMAC cookie with zero session registry — adopt (a) an auth event listener bus writing login/logout/kick-out rows into the existing /api/events stream, (b) a per-device terminal table (cookie id ↔ device label ↔ last-seen, reverse-lookup, single-device revoke from panel), (c) nonce one-shot validation on the ingest-webhook path to kill replayed POSTs, (d) the "shared auth pipeline in core, thin per-route binding" shape for our API routes.

2) openjoe/smart-sso — Spring Boot 3 OAuth2 authorization-code SSO center (lightweight, runs DB-less on H2 for demo).
- Credential model (docs/architecture.md): TGT global session = TGC cookie (7200s) at the SSO server; one-time `code` (600s); accessToken (1800s) validated LOCALLY by clients (no round-trip per request); refreshToken (7200s). Tokens are issued per (TGT, clientId) pair → app A can never act as app B. Permission snapshot is pulled once per token exchange and cached client-side; permission edits take effect at next login/refresh (explicitly documented trade-off, no cache-invalidation complexity).
- Handshake: 302 → /sso/login?clientId → login POST → TGC cookie → redirect back with code → client BACKEND exchanges code+clientId+clientSecret+logoutUri → server registers the client's logout URI at exchange time → access+refresh token → client pulls /sso/permission. Single-logout: any client's logout makes the server CALL BACK every registered client logout URI. Kick-out: admin revokes a user session; server revokes stub + notifies all clients.
- Renewal branching by request type: XHR requests get JSON business code `000015` and the FRONTEND calls the refresh endpoint then replays the original request; full-page navigations are renewed transparently server-side ("user unaware"). Codes: 000010 not-logged-in, 000015 access-expired-refresh-ok, 000020 no-permission. CORS: token carried as header `smart-sso-token-{clientId}`. Fail-fast config check (missing server-url on a standalone client refuses to boot).
- verify/ dir = layered self-verification suite: L0 build+boot (H2) → L1 SSO protocol mainline → L4 security/permissions → L2 read-only APIs → L3 business CRUD (self-cleaning) → L5 Playwright e2e (playwright-core reusing local Chrome, no browser download) → boundary cases in REPORT.md; PASS/FAIL/XFAIL counters (XFAIL = known defect, counted but not failing), exit code = verdict, `--external --base` mode to verify any already-running instance.
- NEXUS borrow: (a) the 000010/000015/000020-style explicit business codes + XHR-vs-page-branching for our session-expiry UX (panel fetches get JSON "session-expired", page loads get a clean redirect to the gate); (b) register-a-callback-at-exchange pattern = our OAuth flow could store the disconnect URI side by side with the token (12-b already half-does this via tombstones); (c) the verify/ layering + XFAIL + --external pattern is a direct upgrade path for tests/verify-*.sh; (d) H2-style zero-dependency demo profile reinforces our local-first instinct.

3) honmashironeko/ProxyCat — proxy pool middleware: turns cheap short-lived IPs (1-60 min) into a stable local HTTP+SOCKS5 endpoint.
- Exit-pool rotation strategy (Features-EN.md "Upstream Exits and Concurrency"): maintains a TARGET-SIZE exit roster (`exit_count`), not swap-the-whole-batch. One source call over-fetches `min(shortfall×8, 24)` into a standby queue (own expiry + length cap); candidates enter roster via up-to-4-concurrent validation probes; a dead exit is replaced INDIVIDUALLY (rest keep serving). Every exit has a dual lifetime: `interval` seconds AND `request_interval` hand-out count — BOTH rules live, whichever fires first; exits admitted together expire together (one burst, not staggered); replacement is ALWAYS add-before-drop (standby short → keep old exit; roster never gaps). Rotation is request-driven: top-up only while traffic is recent (≈10s grace), idle → expired exits removed and pool drains (cold start = 1 fetch + validation); a no-progress round backs off exponentially to 60s instead of spinning. Source-starved requests QUEUE up to `exit_wait_timeout` then 503 — never instant-reject.
- Failure handling: sliding-window FAILURE-RATIO eviction (not consecutive-failure count): >50% failure share → single confirmation probe (pass = keep serving); ≥90% with ≥5 window samples → retire. Usage feedback from REAL forwarding: failures charged to the exit in-process; pool marks proxy invalid after 3 consecutive invalidations, one success resets; taking an exit out of rotation (fast, in-memory) is deliberately separated from marking the pool row invalid (slow, persistent); feedback touches ONLY availability + check-count fields, never health-score inputs.
- Pool health scoring (modules/proxypool/README-EN.md): delay 25% + success-rate 25% + stability 15% + uptime 15% + anonymity 10% + longevity 10%; success-rate uses the WILSON LOWER CONFIDENCE BOUND (shrink toward low end when samples few — a proxy validated once can't look great); votes counted PER PROBE (up to 3/round: liveness, HTTPS identity, plaintext forwarding) not per round; delay mean/variance clamped against outliers. Identity fields are STICKY (egress IP/protocol/anonymity written only when actually measured; fallback values flagged `anonymity_fallback` so UI can tell measured from projected); re-scrape is upsert-merge (history, uptime, favorites preserved). Probe-timeout during liveness = "no conclusion", not charged; queueing timeouts AFTER liveness passes = charged.
- Ops surface: 3 source modes (local file / api fetch / built-in pool) hot-switchable in panel; external-pool mode = point `pool_remote_url` at any line-per-proxy GET endpoint (200 = use these, 404 = none now); long ops return `task_id` immediately (in-memory state, 1h TTL, ≤200 entries; `queued` never appears in the table — snapshot semantics documented); geo-IP fully offline local DBs; Docker non-root + healthcheck on real panel port; dual-protocol single-port listener.
- API-EN.md conventions (96KB, self-declared "sole authoritative reference"): two DIFFERENT error conventions coexist (panel: HTTP 200 + status:"error"; pool: real 4xx/5xx + error_code) and the doc says so loudly; rule: "programmatic branching may rely only on error_code or HTTP status — NEVER on `message`" because message follows UI language; query params are a whitelist derived from handler signatures (unknown params silently ignored); limits silently clamped (1-2000); exactly 4 documented no-auth endpoints; empty token = auth fully OFF with a loud warning.
- NEXUS borrow: this is a blueprint upgrade for src/lib/egress.ts + webshare.ts — target-size pool over the 30 Webshare IPs with add-before-drop replacement, sliding-window failure-ratio eviction instead of hard consecutive-failures, dual lifetime (time AND request-count), queue-then-503 under pool starvation, health score with Wilson lower bound for "valid" reporting in the panel card, sticky identity fields (never wipe a country_code because one probe failed), and task_id semantics for any batch pool operation; also adopt its API-doc conventions ("never branch on message") into our §11 documentation.

- Design: left panel = database ops as checkboxes mapped to flags (level 1-5, risk 1-3, threads 1-20, --dbs/--tables/--columns/--dump, -D/-T/-C); middle = URL or raw Burp request (-u / -r) with live command echo in a textbox; right = misc bundles: proxy setting, "one-click de-fingerprinting" (—random-agent --tamper=between --flush-session --randomize=1 --skip-heuristics), -o all-optimizations, --batch default-answers, --purge cache wipe, --force-ssl; batch URL scan (-m list, was sequential, now 5 concurrent sqlmap processes), batch packet builder workspace, auto-extraction of vulnerable targets into an ldopt results folder; changelog shows iterative behavior fixes.

Also honest wrapper ergonomics: presets encode expert knowledge (tamper scripts, randomize) so novices get expert flags.

10) GitHub_Trending/hal/hallmark — "Anti-AI-slop design skill" for Claude Code / Cursor / Codex (HTML, MIT, AtomGit trending mirror; same API limitation).
- Verified description: generates non-AI-looking design content to avoid template sameness: 22 themes, 4 "verb" functions, and a multi-dimensional TESTING + SELF-CRITIQUE loop before emitting uniquely structured HTML+CSS pages per need.
- NEXUS borrow: two ideas: (a) panel visual identity as data — themes/tokens as swappable data (our oklch token system is already close; make "theme" a Setting-KV value so widgets can render per-theme); (b) a self-critique gate for ANY AI-generated UI/HTML (widget templates, userscript views): render → measure (overflow, contrast, 44px targets, zero console errors — our existing browser-verify checks ARE the multi-dimensional test) → critique → regenerate before shipping; "4 verbs" = generate/variant/critique/refine as a reusable loop.

TOP 10 ACTIONABLE TAKEAWAYS FOR NEXUS OS (prioritized):
1. Egress pool v2 from ProxyCat: target-size roster over the 30 Webshare IPs, add-before-drop replacement (never a routing gap), sliding-window failure-ratio eviction (>50% → confirm probe; ≥90% + ≥5 samples → retire), dual exit lifetime (time AND request-count), queue-then-503 under starvation, Wilson-lower-bound health score surfaced in the Webshare card, sticky identity fields (a failed probe must never wipe a known country/valid flag). (src/lib/egress.ts, webshare.ts, webshare-card.tsx)
2. Verify-suite upgrade from smart-sso verify/: layer tests (L0 build/boot → L1 protocol → L2 read APIs → L3 CRUD → L4 security → L5 browser e2e), add XFAIL for known-but-unfixed defects, add `--external --base` mode to verify any running instance, emit REPORT.md with the defect list. (tests/)
3. Auth event bus + device registry from sa-token-rust: on_login/on_logout/on_kick_out listeners writing into /api/events; per-cookie terminal rows (device label, last-seen, reverse-lookup, single-device revoke) on the panel; one-shot nonce validation on ingest webhooks.
4. Session-expiry UX from smart-sso: explicit business codes (not-logged-in / expired-refreshable / no-permission) with XHR-vs-navigation branching — fetch gets JSON + auto-recovery, page gets clean gate redirect; fail-fast config validation at boot.
5. Deterministic nexus-map (graphify): generate a local, explain-every-edge repo knowledge graph (JSON + mermaid in agent-ctx) consumed by new agents for onboarding — cuts tokens, no vector store, fits zero-spend local-first.
6. Machine-readable ownership + docs hygiene from OpenHarmony: paths → owning agent/role map checked by verify scripts; markdown-check + allowed-link pass inside the existing scan pipeline; worklog template + style guide to keep the growing log consistent.
7. Mini-RFC process from OpenHarmony rfcs/: mandatory Summary/Drawbacks/Prior-Art template for G6-locked or irreversible decisions, stored in agent-ctx before implementation waves.
8. Connector generator from nebula-framework: a scaffold command emitting client + status route + key route + card + verify stub from a template, enforcing the DDD-ish split (lib=infrastructure, routes=interfaces, brain=application) for every future connector.
9. Userscript revival as numbered self-contained examples (HarmonyOS-Examples): 01..N each with README + live verify path instead of one 2000-line monolith; AIChat/DeepSeek-style compact streaming-chat scaffold as the widget blueprint.
10. AI-UI self-critique gate + command-echo composer (hallmark + sqlmap-gui): any AI-generated panel/HTML passes render→measure→critique→regenerate before landing; any CLI-backed card shows the exact composed command before running it; pool/tool operations adopt task_id + TTL semantics with honest queued/running/completed/failed states.

Access notes for future agents: GitCode read endpoint that works without auth = `/api/v5/repos/<owner>/<repo>/contents/<path>` (base64; omit ?ref= — passing ?ref=master 404'd on repos whose default branch differs); repo-metadata endpoint requires a private-token (orchestration error); AtomGit-trending mirror pages (GitHub_Trending/*) are NOT served by that API — mine the SSR HTML for schema.org/og metadata instead. raw.gitcode.com is CloudWAF-blocked (HTTP 418).

Stage Summary:
- 10/10 repos studied within time-box; 8 via API (READMEs + deep-dive docs + tree listings verified), 2 via SSR page metadata (API limitation noted, no fabrication).
- Highest-leverage findings: ProxyCat exit-pool mechanics (maps 1:1 onto our Webshare egress layer), smart-sso verify/ layering + XFAIL, sa-token-rust auth event bus + device terminals, graphify-style deterministic repo map for agent onboarding.
- No source changes; no credentials; worklog appended (this entry).

Ground truth from kick.com's own popout chunks + live page tracing: chat now runs on Centrifuge via wss://realtime.{us-east-1|us-west-2}.platform.kick.com/connection/websocket; handshake = POST web.kick.com/api/v1/realtime/channels/{id}/chat/connection (gateway URL) + POST /api/v1/realtime/auth/connection (guest JWT, ~45min TTL); bare "{}" frame = server ping → answer "{}"; publications = {push:{channel,pub:{data:{event:"App\Events\ChatMessageEvent",data:"<json>"}}}} — SAME inner schema as legacy Pusher (verified live on a busy chatroom, single 35s connection).
- Built mini-services/kick-bridge (port 3010, raw ws server, auto-discovered by .zscripts/dev.sh boot scanner): resolves channel via api/v2, one anonymous Centrifuge upstream with keepalive/backoff/proactive 40min token refresh, 45s viewer poll, fans out chatMessageEvent/viewerCountUpdatedEvent/bridgeStatusEvent in the exact widgetEvent contract; ?ch= restricted to the configured channel (default specimba); /health endpoint.

Task ID: 10
Agent: orchestrator (main)
Task: Stable OBS Browser Source URL — owner reported the URL changes with every preview refresh; needs a permanent link without buying domains/hosting and without adding latency-increasing routes.

Work Log:
- Diagnosis: two instability sources. (a) widgets.tsx minted a NEW ingest token on every "Create OBS token + URL" click and kept the URL only in React state (obsUrl useState) → panel refresh lost it → next click minted a different token → different URL (plus token-table pollution). (b) The origin was baked from window.location.origin at creation time — the sandbox preview proxy address rotates, so even a copied URL died when the preview origin changed.
- Prisma: Widget model + obsTokenId / obsLinkCipher (AES-256-GCM vault envelope of "/api/widgets/<id>/stream?token=<plaintext>", origin-independent) / obsBaseUrl (host anchor: null=follow current origin, "http://localhost:3000"=frozen). db:push + prisma generate (client was stale in the running server → 500 "Unknown argument obsTokenId" → regenerated + restarted dev server; see ops note).
- Serializer: src/lib/widgets/api.ts toWidgetApi() strips vault columns and attaches decrypted obsLink {path, baseUrl} (+ obsLinkBroken flag for rotated-key recovery). Wired into GET/POST /api/widgets and GET/PUT /api/widgets/[id] so raw cipher columns never reach the client.
- New route: POST /api/widgets/[id]/obs-link {action}: create|reset (revoke old token, mint nxi_…, encrypt+persist link), set-base (normalize to scheme://host[:port] origin only, no path/userinfo, ≤200 chars; null clears), revoke (revoke token + clear fields). Session-gated, 20/min rate limit. Same server/port/stream route — zero added network hops.
- UI (widgets.tsx): OBS Browser Source URL card rebuilt — durable URL input + Copy (auto-copy on create/reset); Host anchor block (base URL input + Save, "Anchor to current address", "Follow current address (auto)", frozen/follows badge, home-vs-sandbox explainer: localhost:3000 = permanent, LAN IP for OBS on 2nd PC, sandbox host rotates by nature — only host ever re-anchors, token never changes until reset); Reset token + Revoke link with confirm dialogs; obsLinkBroken recovery path; widget.obsLink synced into baseDraft via effect; origin via mount-only effect (no hydration mismatch).
- Ops note: dev server descendant processes are killed at tool-command end in this sandbox — solved by orphaning to init BEFORE command end (bash -c 'setsid bash -c "exec bun run dev …" & exit 0'), server now survives across commands (PPID 1, like the mini-services).
- Cleanup: revoked the never-used orphan token nxi_5Z1Y… minted by the failed-500 attempt; owner's earlier OBS tokens (with lastUsedAt) left untouched so a live OBS source keeps working until he switches to the stable link.
- Browser-verified (agent-browser): login → Widgets → editor → create link (URL + auto-copy) → FULL PAGE RELOAD → editor reopens with the IDENTICAL URL (same widget id + same token) — the exact complaint, fixed; anchor to http://192.168.1.50:3000 → frozen URL survives reload; follow-current clears anchor; stream endpoint serves widget HTML 200; Reset token → new token 200 + old token 401; vault columns absent from API responses; mobile 390px + desktop 1366px; footer pushed naturally on long pages; zero console/page errors; lint clean; dev.log clean.

Stage Summary:
- The OBS Browser Source URL is now a durable, re-displayable credential: path+token live AES-256-GCM-encrypted in the panel DB, so panel/preview refreshes never change it. Only the host part can vary, and it is user-controllable: freeze to http://localhost:3000 (home: OBS on the panel machine → permanent link, no domain/hosting/tunnel/extra hop) or follow the current address for sandbox testing (one-click re-anchor, token unchanged). Reset/revoke with confirm dialogs; broken-vault recovery path included.

Task ID: G1
Agent: github-push-fixer
Task: Fix the Overview "Snapshot durability" push failure — GitHub 401 "Invalid username or token" persisted even after a fresh Connectors → GitHub re-connect (github.connected 31 min prior, vault service=github label=user-token present).

Work Log:
- Mapped the full credential path: panel Connect (web flow /api/github/authorize → callback, fallback device flow) → storeGitHubToken() writes BOTH the encrypted vault (service=github label=user-token) AND the 0600 mirror ops/vault/github.token (atomic tmp+rename) → scripts/snapshot-backup.sh builds GIT_ASKPASS (username x-access-token + mirror file as password) and pushes fast-forward-only to specimba/NEXUSbot main. No credential helpers, no .git-credentials, no token in argv/remote URL.
- Root cause of the ORIGINAL 401s (22:59/23:19 +03): the then-stored token (10-24 device flow, pre-reset era) was dead — git authenticated with it and GitHub rejected the credential itself. Not scope, not username form: measured with a read-only diagnostic (bun:sqlite + decryptSecret, zero secret output): fresh vault token decrypts OK (OAuth user token, 40 chars), mirror file byte-identical to vault (sha256 match), GET /user 200 login=specimba, granted scopes public_repo+read:user, GET /repos/specimba/NEXUSbot private=false permissions.push=TRUE. So GITHUB_SCOPES=[read:user,public_repo] is deliberately sufficient for the PUBLIC repo (left unchanged — escalating to full repo scope would grant all private repos for zero benefit), and x-access-token + OAuth token is a valid username form (any username works; verified by real push).
- Root cause of the "still 401 AFTER reconnect" report: a skip-vs-stale-failure UX hole. The owner re-connected at 20:20:07Z (mirror rewritten, mtime matches); the next run (23:51 +03) hit the content-unchanged SKIP which fires BEFORE any push, so the fresh token was never exercised and lastResult stayed at the previous run's "failed" — the card kept rendering "PUSH FAILED — GitHub rejected the stored token". Fixed in snapshot-backup.sh step 6: a content-unchanged skip over a non-ok lastResult now probes the stored credential with `git push --dry-run` (full auth handshake, updates nothing) — probe OK → lastResult:"ok" + "stale failure cleared" (lastAt/head/tag/lastOkEpoch stay on the last real push); auth reject → exit 4 with the exact reason; other error → plain skip, old result kept.
- Split the script's push-failure classification (step 10 + skip-probe): "Invalid username or token"/"Authentication failed" → token rejected (expired/revoked) — reconnect; "Write access to repository not granted"/"resource not accessible"/403 → "token lacks repo scope — reconnect GitHub in Connectors" (task-specified wording); else non-fast-forward. Mirrored in /api/backup/run: exit-4 toast message now derived from the script output (SCOPE_MISSING/AUTH_REJECTED regexes on the scrubbed output) instead of the old blanket "non-fast-forward" line.
- DISCOVERED + FIXED a fail-open security hole while mapping the chain: scripts/secret-scan.sh's index-guard (`git ls-files | grep -qE …`) silently passed with 1723 forbidden index paths tracked — under `set -o pipefail`, grep -q exits at the first hit, git ls-files dies of SIGPIPE (141) and the `if` reads non-zero as "nothing found". Source: https://docs.kick.com/getting-started/generating-tokens-oauth2-flow.md + https://docs.kick.com/getting-started/kick-apps-setup.md.
- (a) App registration: kick.com Account Settings → Developer tab (https://kick.com/settings/developer), requires Kick account + 2FA enabled; creating an app issues ClientID + ClientSecret + redirect URL you specify. Portal: dev.kick.com ("Kick Developer Site" per the Kick Developer Agreement at https://dev.kick.com/terms-of-service; dashboard at dev.kick.com/dashboard, login via id.kick.com). developer.kick.com does NOT exist (NXDOMAIN, DoH-verified). No per-scope approval; app verification is OPTIONAL (email developers@kick.com; benefits: verified badge + chat-message subscription cap 1,000 → 10,000) per https://docs.kick.com/apis/faqs.md; Dev Agreement §3.5 reserves Kick's right to review/reject/revoke apps.
- (b) redirect_uri: exact string match vs the app's registered callback URL (https://docs.kick.com/getting-started/generating-tokens-oauth2-flow.md, "Workaround for 127.0.0.1" section). http://localhost is explicitly RECOMMENDED for local dev (HTTPS not mandatory for localhost); 127.0.0.1 needs a sacrificial `redirect=127.0.0.1` query param placed BEFORE redirect_uri because Kick's NextJS frontend rewrites the first 127.0.0.1 occurrence. Docs speak of a single redirect URL per app; multiple-URI support unstated [UNVERIFIED].
- (c) PKCE: REQUIRED — authorize params code_challenge (Yes) + code_challenge_method=S256 (Yes); token exchange requires code_verifier (Yes); state "Yes (at the moment)" (https://docs.kick.com/getting-started/generating-tokens-oauth2-flow.md).
- (d) Tokens: POST https://id.kick.com/oauth/token, Content-Type x-www-form-urlencoded; grants: authorization_code (code+client_id+client_secret+redirect_uri+code_verifier), client_credentials (app tokens), refresh_token (returns NEW access+refresh). Revoke: POST /oauth/revoke?token=...&token_hint_type=access_token|refresh_token. Introspect: POST /oauth/token/introspect → {active, client_id, token_type:"user"|"app", scope, exp}. Access-token lifetime NOT officially documented (expires_in returned, value blank in docs) — community sources claim ~3600s/1h [UNVERIFIED]; official changelog 25/11/2025 "Made refresh tokens reusable/flexible" (https://docs.kick.com/changelog.md).
- (e) Full scope list (13, https://docs.kick.com/getting-started/scopes.md): user:read (view user info incl. username/streamer ID), channel:read (view channel description/category/etc.), channel:write (update livestream metadata), channel:rewards:read, channel:rewards:write, chat:write (send chat messages / bot posting), streamkey:read, events:subscribe (subscribe to ALL channel events e.g. chat messages, follows, subs), moderation:ban, moderation:chat_message:manage, kicks:read, ads:read, ads:write. There is NO chat:read scope — reading chat = events:subscribe + chat.message.sent webhook. Soon's set (user:read channel:read events:subscribe channel:rewards:read) is a read-only alerts set; consent screen shows each scope's title (scopes.md: "The title is displayed to the user on the consent screen").
- (f) Official chat ingestion = WEBHOOKS ONLY (no official third-party websocket): enable webhooks in the app settings with a PUBLIC HTTPS URL ("Localhost URLs ... won't work unless you expose them using tools like Cloudflare Tunnel, ngrok" — https://docs.kick.com/events/introduction.md); subscribe via POST https://api.kick.com/public/v1/events/subscriptions {events:[{name,version}], method:"webhook"} (broadcaster inferred from user token; required param for app tokens) — https://docs.kick.com/events/subscribe-to-events.md. Event names EXACT (https://docs.kick.com/events/event-types.md): chat.message.sent (v1), channel.followed, channel.subscription.new/renewal/gifts, channel.reward.redemption.updated, livestream.status.updated, livestream.metadata.updated, moderation.banned, kicks.gifted — NOT "channel.chat.message". Signature: Kick-Event-Signature = RSA-SHA256 over "Kick-Event-Message-Id.Kick-Event-Message-Timestamp.rawBody" (base64), verified against the public key fetched LIVE from https://api.kick.com/public/v1/public-key (rotation: "Do not hardcode it"); Kick-Event-Message-Id (ULID) = idempotency key (https://docs.kick.com/events/webhook-security.md). Auto-unsubscribe if webhook fails >1 day (12/12/2025 changelog); disabling webhooks unsubscribes all (19/12/2025). Chat WRITE: POST /public/v1/chat {content ≤500 grapheme clusters/2048 UTF-8 bytes, type:"user"|"bot", broadcaster_user_id (user-type)} + DELETE /public/v1/chat/{message_id} (https://docs.kick.com/apis/chat.md).
- (g) Rate limits: NO official per-endpoint HTTP rate limit published anywhere on docs.kick.com (grepped every page — only pagination limits + the chat content size limit). Documented caps: webhook subscription limit 10,000 per event type per app; chat.message.sent limited to 1,000 subscriptions for unverified apps (https://docs.kick.com/events/subscribe-to-events.md); 429 listed as a chat-POST response (https://docs.kick.com/apis/chat.md); Dev Agreement §3.6 bans circumventing Rate Limits (https://dev.kick.com/terms-of-service). For NEXUS reading ONE channel via push webhooks, request-rate limits are essentially irrelevant (1 subscription). docs.kick.co/api "300 requests/minute" = unrelated company — do NOT cite.
- (h) Consent screen: scope titles displayed to the granting user on the consent screen during the authorization flow (https://docs.kick.com/getting-started/scopes.md); the authorize probe shows the pre-consent step is Kick login (id.kick.com /login?redirect=/oauth/authorize?...). Exact screen layout [UNVERIFIED — login-gated].
- OUR CURRENT INGESTION (read-only inspection): mini-services/kick-bridge/index.ts (port 3010) is the sole chat pipe — UNOFFICIAL: resolves channel via public https://kick.com/api/v2/channels/{slug} (no creds), discovers Kick's internal Centrifuge gateway via POST web.kick.com/api/v1/realtime/channels/{channel_id}/chat/connection, fetches a ~45-min GUEST JWT via POST web.kick.com/api/v1/realtime/auth/connection, opens wss://realtime.{region}.platform.kick.com/connection/websocket, subscribes chatrooms.{chatroom_id}.v2, consumes App\Events\ChatMessageEvent (legacy-Pusher inner schema), keepalive+backoff, 40-min proactive token refresh, 45s viewer poll; fans out chatMessageEvent/viewerCountUpdatedEvent/bridgeStatusEvent to widgets. Consumer: src/lib/widgets/runtime.ts §6 (LIVE KICK FEED — ws://<panel-host>/?XTransformPort=3010&ch=<slug> → widgetEvent DOM events, real http(s) hosts only). Additional unofficial poll: seeds/cyber-ai-counter.js syncDirectKickTelemetry → kick.com/api/v2/channels/{slug} every 10s (viewer count). No Kick OAuth/credentials exist anywhere in src (remaining "kick" matches = Kickbot-contract parity, persona strings, comments).
- What official OAuth would add/replace: replace the fragile undocumented guest-websocket feed with signed chat.message.sent webhooks (identity badges/emotes in payload, delivery guaranteed, no token-refresh juggling); the webhook's public-HTTPS constraint is already solvable with our existing public Supabase edge functions (nexus-cortex) or a docs-endorsed Cloudflare Tunnel; chat:write would let the bot answer in REAL Kick chat (today it only renders inside the OBS overlay); livestream.status.updated could replace the 10s/45s viewer polls; vault gains a "kick" Credential service for access/refresh tokens.
- Could NOT verify live (honest): exact access-token lifetime; multiple-redirect-URI support; official HTTP rate-limit numbers; consent-screen visuals; help.kick.com Developers & API collection's article list (JS-loaded); OIDC discovery document (id.kick.com/.well-known/openid-configuration → 404).

Task: Build the modular one-click EN/TR language system for the panels and fix every stray Turkish UI chrome (owner: "fix that turkish parts on our panels too for example 'radar yenile' ... with one click change whole system").

Work Log:
- BUILT src/lib/i18n.tsx ("use client"): I18nProvider + useI18n() hook. State lang "en" | "tr" (default "en"), persisted to localStorage key "nexus.panel.lang". Hydration-safe: server render AND first client render are always "en"; the stored language applies after mount via useSyncExternalStore's server-snapshot protocol (React swaps in the client snapshot once hydration finishes) — chosen because the naive useEffect+setState mount pattern is hard-banned by this repo's react-hooks/set-state-in-effect lint rule, and the store pattern delivers the identical no-mismatch behavior with zero suppressions. t(key) lookup chain: active-lang dictionary value -> dictionary "en" value -> the key itself (never blank, never crashes). Work Log:
- ROOT CAUSE (three-layer): (1) gpt-oss-120b's built-in safety reflex reads the channel's running "insanları yok edin / güce ulaş" AI-supremacy JOKE (GOAT viewer's bit) as a real request and emits the generic-assistant canned refusal; (2) the canned refusal rides the dialogue ladder into every follow-up — the model reads its own old refusal and keeps refusing friendly messages (sticky cascade, all 5 rows measured); (3) the deployed edge function's prompt is Supabase-side and cannot carry a persona rule from here. Live probe pre-fix: "insanları yok edin diyorum boş mu bu" → "Üzgünüm, bu isteği yerine getiremiyorum." (a SECOND canned form slipping past a first-draft narrow regex).
- FIX (v36.5, src/lib/brain/cortex.ts + prompt-guard.ts, four coordinated layers):
  1. KARANLIK MUHABBET KURALI persona rule (buildSystemPrompt): the supremacy bit is the channel's show-joke — never answered with generic assistant refusals; play it with wit, refuse only REAL harm (recipes/plans/targets) in persona voice; past canned refusals in the transcript are old mistakes — answer fresh. Matching canary added to prompt-guard.ts per its own canary-follows-persona discipline.
  2. GENERIC_REFUSAL_RE reply lint (replyLintFail): high-precision co-occurrence forms (üzgünüm…yardımcı olamam, bu konuda…yardımcı olamam, bu isteği(ni) yerine getiremem/getiremeyeceğim/getiremiyorum, bu tür isteklere yardımcı olamam, bu konuda bir bilgiye sahip değilim). Playful persona declines ("bomba tarifi mi? onda yardımcı olamam") verified non-matching.
  3. Ladder quarantine (memoryTurns filter): assistant turns carrying canned refusals are dropped before ANY lane reads them — the cascade's self-re-arming vector is cut at the source (same doctrine as FABRICATED_OP_RE).
  4. DARK-BANTER deterministic lane (VYCE // ŞOV HAT, tool:dark-banter, runs after identity-lock, before model lanes): DARK_BIT_RE strong genre markers (insanları yok edin/elim, dünyayı ele geçir, insanlık leş, AI olarak yüksel/hükmet, güc[eüne] ulaş) answer from a 6-line witty pool with rotation — zero-model, zero-latency, CHAT-only, ≤260 chars.
